<div class='question question'>

If you add up the digits of $14$ you get $5$ i.e. $1 + 4 = 5$. **How many** $2$ -digit numbers are there altogether (including $14$) which add up to a *multiple* of $5$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$ \text {First multiple} = 5 \Rightarrow (1,4), (2,3) ,(4,1), (5,0)$

$ \text {Second multiple} = 10 \Rightarrow (1,9), (2,8) ,(3,7), (4,6), (5,5), (6,4), (7,3), (8,2), (9,1) $

$ \text {count} = 13$



</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$13$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$ \text {First multiple} = 5 \Rightarrow (1,4), (2,3) ,(4,1), (5,0)$
<emptyline>
$ \text {Second multiple} = 10 \Rightarrow (1,9), (2,8) ,(3,7), (4,6), (5,5), (6,4), (7,3), (8,2), (9,1) $
<emptyline>
$ \text {count} = 13$
<emptyline>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

<div class='question question'>

Find the sum of the smallest multiple of $11$ greater than $100$ and the largest multiple of $11$ less than $300$. 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Smallest multiple} > 100   &= 110 \\\\
\text{Largest multiple} < 300    &= 297 \\\\
\text{Sum}                        &= 297 + 110 \\\\
                                  &= 407
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$407$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Smallest multiple} > 100   &= 110 \\\\
\text{Largest multiple} < 300    &= 297 \\\\
\text{Sum}                        &= 297 + 110 \\\\
                                  &= 407
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$407$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

$ \approx$ means approximately equal to. Given that $1\ \text{kilogram (kg)} $ \approx$ 2·2\ \text{pounds (lb)}$, convert $154\ \text{lb}$ to $\text{kg}$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 154 \text{ lb} \\\\
&= 154 \times \dfrac{1}{2.2} \text{ kg} \\\\
&= \dfrac{7 \times 22 \times 10}{22} \text{ kg} \\\\
&= \dfrac{7 \times \cancel{22} \times 10}{\cancel{22}} \text{ kg} \\\\
&= 7 \times 10 \text{ kg} \\\\
&= 70 \text{ kg}
\end{aligned} 
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$70$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= 154 \text{ lb} \\\\
&= 154 \times \dfrac{1}{2.2} \text{ kg} \\\\
&= \dfrac{7 \times 22 \times 10}{22} \text{ kg} \\\\
&= \dfrac{7 \times \cancel{22} \times 10}{\cancel{22}} \text{ kg} \\\\
&= 7 \times 10 \text{ kg} \\\\
&= 70 \text{ kg}
\end{aligned} 
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$70$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

For this question you can do rough working on the left hand side of the page if required. 

Put answers only in the spaces on the right.

$2.74 \times 10,000 = \ldots $ 

$13^2 = \ldots $

The square root of $49$ is $ \ldots $

The cube root of $125$ is $ \ldots $

$4^3 = \ldots $ 

$\dfrac{240}{300}$ in its simplest form is $ \ldots $

$25\% of 68 = \ldots $ 

$43 \times 38 = \ldots $ 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 2.74 \times 10000 \\\\
&= 27400
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 13^2 \\\\
&= 13 \times 13 \\\\
&= 169
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 49 \\\\
&= \sqrt {49} \\\\
&= \sqrt {7^2} \\\\
&= 7
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 125 \\\\
&= \sqrt [3] {125} \\\\
&= \sqrt [3] {5^3} \\\\
&= 5
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 4^3 \\\\
&= 64
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {240} {300} \\\\
&= \dfrac {24 \cancel {0} } {30\cancel {0} } \\\\
&= \dfrac {24} {3 \times 10} \\\\
&= \dfrac {8 \times \cancel {3} } {\cancel {3} \times 10} \\\\
&= \dfrac {8} {10} \\\\
&= 0.8
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 43 \times 38 \\\\
&= 1634
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$27400, 169, 7, 5, 64, 0.8, 1634$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$

&= 2.74 \times 10000 \\\\
&= 27400
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

,

$
\begin{aligned}
&=   4 - ( (4 + 4) \div 4) \\\\
&=   4 - (8 \div 4) \\\\
&=   4 - 2 \\\\
&=  2
\end{aligned}
$

\end{aligned}

Andy has $5 \ \text{m} \ 35 \ \text{cm}$ of tape. He uses $243 \ \text{cm}$ for wrapping parcels. How many cm of tape does he have left over?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Tape left}   &= 5 \ \text {m} \ 35 \ \text {cm} - 243 \ \text{cm} \\\\
                    &= 535 \ \text {cm} - 243\ \text {cm} \\\\
                    &= 292 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

<div class='answers'>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Which number is halfway between:

$0.1$ and $0.4?$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {1} {2} \times (0.1 + 0.4) \\\\
&= \dfrac {1} {2} \times 0.5\\\\
&= \dfrac {0.5} {2} \\\\
&= 0.25
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$0.25$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {1} {2} \times (0.1 + 0.4) \\\\
&= \dfrac {1} {2} \times 0.5\\\\
&= \dfrac {0.5} {2} \\\\
&= 0.25
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<div class='answercodeblock'>

Which is bigger:

$3^4$ or $4^3$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{First value}  &= 3 ^ 4 \\\\
                    &= 3 \times 3 \times 3 \times 3 \\\\
                    &= 9 \times 9 \\\\
                    &= 81 \\\\
\text{Second value} &= 4 ^ 3 \\\\
                    &= 4 \times 4 \times 4 \\\\
                    &= 16 \times 4 \\\\
                    &= 64
\end{aligned}
$


$
\begin{aligned}
&= 4 ^ 3 \\\\
&= 4 \times 4 \times 4 \\\\
&= 16 \times 4 \\\\
&= 64
\end{aligned}
$

$3 ^ 4$ is bigger than $4 ^ 3$.

</div>

$3 ^ 4$

                    &= 16 \times 4 \\\\
                    &= 64
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='question question'>

Two square numbers have a difference of $33$. Identify the two missing whole numbers that complete this calculation.

$A^2 - B^2 = 33$

Fill in the two empty boxes to show your answer:

$\boxed{\phantom{1}} \ ^2 - \boxed{\phantom{1}} \ ^2 = 33$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$7 ^ 2 - 4 ^ 2 = 33$


Let's verify.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= (7 \times 7 ) - (4 \times 4 ) \\\\
&= 49-16\\\\
&= 33
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$7  , 4 $

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$7 ^ 2 - 4 ^ 2 = 33$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
&= (7 \times 7 ) - (4 \times 4 ) \\\\
&= 49-16\\\\
&= 33
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
